Now I have gathered comprehensive information. Let me create a thorough summary document:

## Task Resolution Flow in Desloppify - Comprehensive Exploration Report

Based on my thorough exploration of the desloppify codebase, here's the complete task resolution flow:

---

### 1. How Users Mark Tasks as "Resolved" or "Done"

**Entry Point: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`**

The `cmd_resolve()` function is the main entry point. Users invoke it through the CLI:

```bash
desloppify resolve <patterns> --status <status> [--note <text>] [--attest <text>]
```

**Status values:**
- `fixed` - Finding is fixed (requires attestation + 50+ char note)
- `wontfix` - Finding is intentional debt (requires 50+ char note)
- `open` - Reopening a previously resolved finding (no extra requirements)

**Key Validation Checks (from `selection.py`):**
1. **Note Length** (line 69-80): For `fixed` status, minimum 50 characters required - describes what was actually done
2. **Attestation Keywords** (line 14-47): For `fixed` status, must contain "i have actually" and "not gaming" to prove human verification
3. **Wontfix Note Requirement** (line 83-88): Wontfix requires a note to explain reasoning
4. **Batch Wontfix Confirmation** (line 136-164): Resolving >10 items as wontfix requires `--confirm-batch-wontfix` flag; estimates strict-score debt and warns user
5. **Queue Order Guard** (from `queue_guard.py`): If a living plan exists with `queue_order`, items being resolved as `fixed` must be at the front of the queue - prevents out-of-order resolution

---

### 2. What Happens After Resolution

**State Layer Changes (from `engine/_state/resolution.py`)**

When `resolve_findings()` is called:

1. **Status Update** (line 143-157): Sets finding status, clears suppression, records resolution timestamp and attestation
2. **Wontfix Snapshot** (line 116-125): For wontfix status, captures current scan count, tier, confidence, and detail as a snapshot
3. **Reopening Logic** (line 126-141): For reopening as `open`, increments `reopen_count`, clears wontfix metadata, records manual reopen attestation
4. **Subjective Assessment Staling** (line 40-80): When review findings are resolved, their related subjective dimensions are marked with `needs_review_refresh=True` and `stale_since` timestamp - signals to re-run review

**Plan Integration (from `resolve/cmd.py` lines 84-102)**

If a living plan exists:
- **`purge_ids()`** (from `engine/_plan/operations.py` line 540): Removes resolved IDs from:
  - `queue_order` list
  - `skipped` dict
  - All cluster memberships
  - Clears stale cluster references in overrides (preserves notes/descriptions)
- **`append_log_entry()`** (line 87-95): Records structured execution log entry with:
  - Finding IDs resolved
  - Status (fixed/wontfix/open)
  - Note and attestation text
  - Timestamp and actor ("user")
- **`save_plan()`**: Persists plan changes

**Scoring Recomputation** (from `resolution.py` line 169)**

After resolution state updates, `_recompute_stats()` is called to update:
- Total finding counts
- Dimension scores
- Overall/objective/strict scores

**Query Output Update (from `apply.py` line 70-91)**

A resolve query entry is written to `.desloppify/query.json` recording:
- Patterns matched
- Resolution status
- Count of IDs resolved
- Score deltas (before/after)
- Narrative context

---

### 3. Post-Resolution User Feedback

**Display Summary (from `render.py`)**

1. **Resolution Summary** (line 16-22): Green banner showing "Resolved N finding(s) as {status}" with first 20 IDs listed
2. **Wontfix Debt Warning** (line 25-54): Yellow warning if batch wontfix, shows wontfix count and percentage of actionable findings
3. **Score Movement Display** (from `queue_progress.py` - called line 110): Shows:
   - Overall/objective/strict/verified scores with deltas
   - Strict target nudge if gap remains
   - Strict gap note: "wontfix items still count against strict score"
4. **Subjective Reset Hint** (line 103-149): If review findings resolved, suggests re-running review for updated dimension scores:
   ```
   Subjective scores unchanged — re-run review for updated scores: <dims>
   ```
5. **Milestone Banner** (line 124-125): Green milestone message if narrative detects phase progression (computed by `narrative_mod.compute_narrative()`)
6. **Next Command Hint** (line 152-171): Tells user what to do next:
   - If remaining open findings: "Run `desloppify next`"
   - If no remaining findings: "Run `desloppify scan`"

---

### 4. How the `next` Command Works

**Entry Point: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/next.py`**

The `cmd_next()` function displays the highest-priority item to work on next.

**Workflow:**

1. **Load State** (line 70-75): Ensures a completed scan exists
2. **Load Plan** (line 182-189): Loads living plan if it has queue_order/overrides/clusters
3. **Effective Cluster Resolution** (line 192-196): If plan has `active_cluster` set and no explicit scope/cluster arg, auto-scopes to that cluster
4. **Build Work Queue** (line 198-212): Calls `build_work_queue()` with options:
   - `count`: Number of items to retrieve (default 1)
   - `plan`: Living plan for plan-aware ranking
   - `scope`: Optional file path filter
   - `cluster`: Optional cluster focus
   - `include_subjective`: Include low-scored subjective dimensions
   - `subjective_threshold`: Target strict score (default 95.0 from config)
5. **Compute Narrative** (line 217-220): Calls `compute_narrative()` with command="next" context
6. **Render Queue Items** (line 247-275): Displays:
   - Queue header with summary stats
   - Terminal items with formatting (medium/high confidence badges)
   - Single-item resolution hints
   - Followup nudges (stale subjective dimensions, strict target progress, etc.)

**Queue Item Types:**

- **Regular findings**: Code issues with confidence level, detector type, file path, summary
- **Cluster items**: Group of related findings (shown if plan has clusters)
- **Workflow stage items**: Triage stages (observe/reflect/organize/recommend) - blocked until dependencies complete
- **Subjective dimension items**: Low-scoring subjective assessments needing re-review

**Plan-Aware Behavior:**

When a plan exists, the work queue respects:
- **Queue order** (`plan.queue_order`): Items ranked by their position in this list
- **Skipped items** (`plan.skipped`): Items temporarily or permanently skipped are filtered out
- **Cluster focus** (`plan.active_cluster`): Auto-focuses queue to cluster members if set
- **Overrides** (`plan.overrides`): Description/note annotations shown in queue items
- **Plan start strict score**: Frozen baseline for progress display against target

---

### 5. Complete User Experience Flow

```
USER WORKFLOW:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. desloppify scan [--path <dir>]                               │
│    ↓ Scans codebase, detects issues, saves state & creates plan │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 2. desloppify plan [triage/cluster/move/show]                   │
│    ↓ Triages review findings, organizes queue, sets focus       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 3. desloppify next [--count N] [--explain] [--cluster C]        │
│    ↓ Shows highest-priority item or cluster to work on          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 4. [User works on the issue]                                    │
│    ↓ Fixes code, updates tests, resolves the finding            │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 5. desloppify resolve <pattern> --status fixed                  │
│      --note "..."                  (50+ chars explaining work)   │
│      --attest "I have actually ... not gaming ..."              │
│    ↓ Marks finding as resolved, updates plan, shows score delta │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 6a. IF findings remain:                                         │
│     → "desloppify next" (repeat from step 3)                    │
│                                                                  │
│ 6b. IF no findings remain:                                      │
│     → "desloppify scan" (re-scan to verify and find new issues) │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Alternate Paths:**

- **Wontfix Resolution**: `resolve <pattern> --status wontfix --note "..." --confirm-batch-wontfix` (if >10 items)
  - Adds to hidden debt (counts in overall score but shows in strict score gap)
  - Records permanent decision for future review

- **Reopening**: `resolve <pattern> --status open [--note "..."]`
  - Reopens previously resolved finding
  - Increments reopen_count
  - Records manual reopen attestation

- **Queue Management**: 
  - `desloppify plan move <pattern> --position top|bottom|before <target>`
  - `desloppify plan skip <pattern> --reason "..."` (temporarily defer)
  - `desloppify plan focus <pattern>` (auto-scope next/show to cluster)

---

### 6. Key Architecture Insights

**State Layer Concerns:**
- Finding status mutations happen in `engine/_state/resolution.py` - pure functions operating on state dict
- Scoring recomputation is stateless - `_recompute_stats()` recalculates from findings
- Subjective assessment staleness is metadata on the assessment (needs_review_refresh flag)

**Plan Layer Concerns:**
- Lives in `engine/_plan/` with facade API in `engine/plan.py`
- Execution log (`execution_log`) records all user actions for audit trail
- Clusters store finding IDs and metadata (auto vs manual)
- Queue order is the canonical prioritization list
- Skipped dict maps finding IDs to skip reasons (temporary/permanent)
- Overrides store user descriptions/notes per finding

**Integration Points:**
- Resolve command enforces queue order guard (must resolve front item first in ordered queue)
- Resolve updates both state AND plan atomically
- Plan's frozen start_strict score enables progress tracking toward target
- Narrative computation uses plan context for phase/strategy/action recommendations

**Guardrails:**
- Triage guardrail blocks "fixed" resolution when new review findings haven't been triaged
  - Bypassable with `--force-resolve --attest "..."` (30+ chars)
- Batch wontfix guard blocks >10 item wontfix batch without `--confirm-batch-wontfix`
  - Shows estimated strict-score debt impact
- Note length validation (50 chars) ensures quality resolution documentation

---

This architecture creates a cohesive workflow where users are guided from scan → planning → task-by-task resolution, with the system preventing common mistakes (out-of-order resolution, undocumented decisions, gaming the score system) while maintaining an audit trail of all actions.

## Summary

Based on my thorough exploration of the desloppify codebase, here is the comprehensive analysis of the plan/state data structures and persistence:

---

## 1. Plan Data Model (schema.py)

The plan data model is defined in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py` as a `PlanModel` TypedDict with the following structure:

```python
class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    version: int                                    # Current: PLAN_VERSION = 7
    created: str                                    # UTC timestamp when plan created
    updated: str                                    # UTC timestamp of last update
    queue_order: list[str]                         # Primary queue of finding IDs in priority order
    deferred: list[str]                            # Kept empty for migration compat
    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry]                 # Findings marked temporary/permanent/false_positive
    active_cluster: str | None                     # Currently focused cluster name
    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]            # Custom descriptions, notes, cluster assignments
    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]                  # Named groupings of findings
    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]        # Findings that disappeared/were remapped
    promoted_ids: list[str]                       # IDs user moved to top (for barrier logic)
    plan_start_scores: dict                        # Frozen score snapshot from plan creation
    execution_log: list[ExecutionLogEntry]        # Audit trail of all operations
    epic_triage_meta: dict                         # Triage engine metadata
```

### Key Sub-Types:

**SkipEntry** (for skipped findings):
- `finding_id`: Required[str]
- `kind`: Required[str] — one of "temporary", "permanent", "false_positive", "triaged_out"
- `reason`: Optional description
- `note`: Required for permanent (wontfix note)
- `attestation`: Required for permanent/false_positive
- `created_at`: UTC timestamp
- `review_after`: N scans before auto-resurface (temporary only)
- `skipped_at_scan`: State.scan_count when skipped

**ItemOverride** (augmentations applied to findings):
- `finding_id`: Required[str]
- `description`: Custom description
- `note`: Custom note
- `cluster`: Cluster name the finding belongs to
- `created_at`: UTC timestamp
- `updated_at`: UTC timestamp

**Cluster** (grouped findings for bulk action):
- `name`: Required[str]
- `description`: Human-readable cluster purpose
- `finding_ids`: list[str] — members of this cluster
- `created_at`: UTC timestamp
- `updated_at`: UTC timestamp
- `auto`: bool — True for auto-generated clusters (auto/*)
- `cluster_key`: Deterministic grouping key for regeneration (e.g., "typed::dict_keys::phantom_read")
- `action`: Primary resolution command/guidance text
- `user_modified`: True when user manually edits membership
- `optional`: True for optional clusters (under-target review)

**SupersededEntry** (findings that disappeared):
- `original_id`: Required[str]
- `original_detector`: str
- `original_file`: str
- `original_summary`: str
- `status`: "superseded" | "remapped" | "dismissed"
- `superseded_at`: UTC timestamp
- `remapped_to`: Candidate finding ID if remapped
- `candidates`: list[str] — up to 5 potential remaps
- `note`: Preserved from override

**ExecutionLogEntry** (audit trail):
- `timestamp`: Required[str] UTC
- `action`: Required[str] — "done", "skip", "unskip", "resolve", "reconcile", "cluster_done", "focus", "reset", "cluster_create", "cluster_add", "cluster_remove", "cluster_delete", "cluster_move", "cluster_update", "cluster_merge"
- `finding_ids`: list[str]
- `cluster_name`: Optional cluster involved
- `actor`: "user" | "system" | "agent"
- `note`: Optional context
- `detail`: dict — action-specific extra data

---

## 2. Operations (operations.py)

Pure mutation functions for the plan model:

**Queue Management:**
- `move_items(plan, finding_ids, position, target, offset)` — Reorder items; tracks promoted_ids when moved to "top"
- `skip_items(plan, finding_ids, kind, reason, note, attestation, review_after, scan_count)` — Move to skipped dict
- `unskip_items(plan, finding_ids)` — Return from skipped; resolves permanent/false_positive IDs needing state reopen
- `resurface_stale_skips(plan, current_scan_count)` — Auto-resurface temporary skips past review_after threshold

**Finding Metadata:**
- `describe_finding(plan, finding_id, description)` — Set/clear augmented description
- `annotate_finding(plan, finding_id, note)` — Set/clear note on finding

**Cluster Operations:**
- `create_cluster(plan, name, description, action)` — Create named cluster; rejects names starting with "auto/" or "epic/"
- `add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)` — Add members; syncs overrides
- `remove_from_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)` — Remove members; marks auto-clusters as user_modified
- `delete_cluster(plan, name)` — Delete cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides
- `merge_clusters(plan, source_name, target_name)` — Move all source findings to target, copy metadata, delete source
- `move_cluster(plan, cluster_name, position, target, offset)` — Move all members as contiguous block

**Focus & Reset:**
- `set_focus(plan, cluster_name)` — Set active_cluster
- `clear_focus(plan)` — Clear active_cluster

**Lifecycle:**
- `reset_plan(plan)` — Clear plan state preserving version and created timestamp
- `purge_ids(plan, finding_ids)` — Remove IDs from queue_order, skipped, cluster memberships (preserves overrides)

**Logging:**
- `append_log_entry(plan, action, finding_ids, cluster_name, actor, note, detail)` — Add to execution_log with cap (default 10000)

---

## 3. State Data Model (schema.py)

State is defined in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py`:

```python
class StateModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    version: int                                    # CURRENT_VERSION = 1
    created: str                                    # UTC timestamp when state created
    last_scan: str | None                          # Last successful scan timestamp
    scan_count: int                                 # Total scans run
    overall_score: float
    objective_score: float
    strict_score: float
    verified_strict_score: float
    stats: StateStats                              # Summary counts by status and tier
    findings: dict[str, Finding]                   # Central finding database by ID
    scan_coverage: dict[str, ScanCoverageRecord]
    score_confidence: dict[str, Any]
    scan_history: list[ScanHistoryEntry]           # Historical scan snapshots
    subjective_integrity: SubjectiveIntegrity      # Anti-gaming metadata
    subjective_assessments: dict[str, SubjectiveAssessment]  # Per-dimension scores
    concern_dismissals: dict[str, ConcernDismissal]
```

**Finding** (normalized finding from any detector):
- `id`: str — unique identifier
- `detector`: str — detector name (e.g., "smells", "review", "security")
- `file`: str — file path
- `tier`: int — 1-4 priority tier
- `confidence`: str — "low", "medium", "high"
- `summary`: str — human-readable summary
- `detail`: dict — detector-specific details (lines, line numbers, dimension, etc.)
- `status`: FindingStatus — "open" | "fixed" | "auto_resolved" | "wontfix" | "false_positive"
- `note`: str | None — custom note
- `first_seen`: str — UTC timestamp
- `last_seen`: str — UTC timestamp
- `resolved_at`: str | None — when marked fixed/wontfix
- `reopen_count`: int — times reopened
- `suppressed`: bool — suppression status
- `suppressed_at`: str | None
- `suppression_pattern`: str | None — matching rule
- `resolution_attestation`: dict[str, str | bool | None] — proof of fix (optional)
- `lang`: str — programming language (optional)
- `zone`: str — file intent zone (optional)

---

## 4. Plan Facade (plan.py)

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/plan.py` re-exports the public API from private submodules:

- Schema, persistence, operations, reconciliation, auto-clustering, stale dimension tracking
- **Key function**: `triage_phase_banner(plan)` — Returns banner when TRIAGE_IDs are in queue

---

## 5. Cluster Representation

Clusters have multiple flavors:

**Manual Clusters:**
- User-created clusters with custom names, descriptions, actions
- Examples: "payment-refactor", "api-redesign"
- `user_modified = False` initially

**Auto-Clusters:**
- Generated by `auto_cluster_findings()` algorithm
- Names follow `auto/` prefix pattern
- `cluster_key` is deterministic (e.g., "typed::dict_keys::phantom_read")
- Regenerated each scan; pruned if no matching findings
- If user edits membership, `user_modified = True` → new members merged only
- Examples: "auto/unused-imports", "auto/review-abstraction_fitness"

**Subjective Dimension Clusters:**
- `auto/initial-review` (unscored dimensions, min size 1)
- `auto/stale-review` (previously scored, now stale)
- `auto/under-target-review` (optional, below target score)

**Cluster Handler Operations** (cluster_handlers.py):
- Create, add, remove, delete, move, show, list, update (description/action), merge
- Warn on >50% overlap with manual clusters
- Resolve cluster names in patterns (ID matching, glob patterns)

---

Located at `~/.desloppify/plan.json` (path: `STATE_DIR / "plan.json"`).

**Persistence API** (persistence.py):
- `load_plan(path=None)` — Load from `PLAN_FILE` or custom path; falls back to backup (.json.bak) on corruption; returns empty_plan() if missing
- `save_plan(plan, path=None)` — Validate, save atomically (temp + os.replace), create backup
- `plan_path_for_state(state_path)` — Derive plan.json path from state file path
- `has_living_plan(path=None)` — Check if plan exists and has user intent (queue_order, overrides, or clusters)

---

## 7. Git-Related Tracking

**NO EXISTING GIT INTEGRATION**: The codebase has NO git-related tracking whatsoever. There are:
- No subprocess calls to git
- No branch metadata tracking
- No commit history integration
- No remote (origin) tracking

The execution_log in the plan provides the audit trail instead. All version control would need to be implemented from scratch.

---

## 8. Key Patterns & Mechanisms

1. **Plan Reconciliation** (reconcile.py):
   - Runs after scan save
   - Handles finding disappearance (moves to superseded)
   - Finds candidates for remapping
   - Preserves override notes
   - Cleans up queue_order, skipped, cluster memberships

2. **Auto-Clustering** (auto_cluster.py):
   - Deterministic `_grouping_key()` computation (detector, dimension, subtype, file)
   - Clusters by: detector name, review dimension, code smell subtype, file (for structural)
   - Description/action generation from metadata
   - Singleton groups filtered out (min_cluster_size=2, except unscored=1)
   - Stale cluster deletion (unless user_modified)
   - Ghost ref repair (overrides pointing to non-existent clusters)

3. **Version Control**:
   - PLAN_VERSION = 7 with migrations for v1→v2 (deferred→skipped), v2→v3 (epics), v3→v4 (unified), v5→v6 (gates), v6→v7 (synthesis→triage naming)
   - `ensure_plan_defaults()` normalizes on load
   - `validate_plan()` checks invariants (no ID in both queue_order and skipped, skip kinds)

4. **Atomic Writes**:
   - `safe_write_text()` used for atomicity
   - `.json.bak` created before overwrite
   - Corruption handled gracefully (load from backup or empty plan)

---

This is a comprehensive, production-grade plan management system with full audit trails, deterministic clustering, finding lifecycle management, and atomic persistence—but zero git integration.

## Comprehensive Desloppify Codebase Exploration Report

Based on my thorough exploration of the desloppify codebase, here are the key findings organized by your requested areas:

---

### 1. **Configuration & Settings Storage** (`desloppify/core/config.py`)

**Configuration File Location:**
- **Primary:** `.desloppify/config.json` in the project root
- **Auto-migration:** Legacy config keys are migrated from state files (`state-*.json`) on first run

**Configuration Schema** (20+ settings):
```
- target_strict_score (int, default: 95) — north-star scoring target
- review_max_age_days (int, default: 30) — stale review detection
- review_batch_max_files (int, default: 80) — batch sizing
- holistic_max_age_days (int, default: 30)
- generate_scorecard (bool, default: True)
- badge_path (str, default: "scorecard.png")
- exclude (list) — path patterns to exclude
- ignore (list) — finding patterns to suppress
- ignore_metadata (dict) — metadata for ignored patterns {pattern: {note, added_at}}
- zone_overrides (dict) — manual zone assignments
- review_dimensions (list) — override default dimensions
- large_files_threshold, props_threshold, finding_noise_budget, execution_log_max_entries
- needs_rescan (bool) — flag when config changes invalidate scores
- languages (dict) — language-specific settings
```

**Key Functions:**
- `load_config()` — loads with auto-migration & fill defaults
- `save_config()` — atomic writes via `safe_write_text()` (temp + os.replace)
- `set_config_value()` — CLI-driven config updates with type coercion
- Config values support special parsing: "never" → 0 for age keys

---

### 2. **File Persistence** (`desloppify/core/file_paths.py`)

**Core Persistence Files:**
- **State:** `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` (language-specific scan results)
- **Plan:** `.desloppify/plan.json` (living prioritized work queue)
- **Config:** `.desloppify/config.json` (settings)
- **Query Output:** `.desloppify/query.json` (machine-readable results)
- **Backups:** `.json.bak` files (atomic write safety)

**Atomic Write Strategy:**
- Uses `safe_write_text()` which:
  1. Creates temp file via `tempfile.mkstemp()` in same directory
  2. Writes content
  3. Atomically replaces original with `os.replace()`
  4. Cleans up temp on failure

**Path Resolution:**
- `rel()` — converts absolute paths to relative (handles fallback for out-of-project paths)
- `resolve_scan_file()` — resolves relative paths against scan_root first, then project root
- `matches_exclusion()` — component-based matching (not substring), supports fnmatch patterns & directory separators

---

### 3. **User Interaction Patterns**

**CLI Framework:**
- **argparse** (no click/typer) — see `desloppify/app/cli_support/parser.py` & `parser_groups_admin.py`
- **Subcommand dispatch:** `app/commands/registry.py` maps command names to handlers
- **Main entry:** `desloppify/cli.py`

**User Input Collection:**
- **Plan triage workflow** (`desloppify/engine/_plan/epic_triage.py`):
  - `collect_triage_input()` — gathers open findings, mechanical context, existing clusters
  - No interactive stdin prompts found; workflow is statement-based with LLM generation
  - **4-stage triage:** observe → reflect → organize → commit (gates require text enrichment)

- **Plan override handlers** (`desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`):
  - `cmd_plan_describe()`, `cmd_plan_note()`, `cmd_plan_skip()`, `cmd_plan_focus()` etc.
  - Text input via argparse (patterns & text arguments), not interactive input()

**No Rich/Click Prompts Found:**
- No `Confirm.ask()`, `Prompt.ask()`, `click.confirm()`, or `click.prompt()` in app/ directory
- Interactive user input appears minimal; most interaction is CLI-driven with args

---

### 4. **Plan Subcommands** (`desloppify/app/commands/plan/cmd.py`)

**Top-level Dispatcher:** `cmd_plan()` routes to:

| Subcommand | Handler | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| `(none)` | `_cmd_plan_generate()` | Generate prioritized markdown plan output |
| `show` | `_cmd_plan_show()` | Display plan metadata summary |
| `queue` | `cmd_plan_queue()` | Show prioritized queue |
| `reset` | `_cmd_plan_reset()` | Clear entire plan |
| `move` | `cmd_plan_move()` | Reorder items |
| `describe` | `cmd_plan_describe()` | Set augmented description |
| `note` | `cmd_plan_note()` | Set notes on findings |
| `focus` | `cmd_plan_focus()` | Set active cluster |
| `skip` | `cmd_plan_skip()` | Mark items temporary/permanent/false-positive |
| `unskip` | `cmd_plan_unskip()` | Restore skipped items |
| `reopen` | `cmd_plan_reopen()` | Resurrect done items |
| `done` | `cmd_plan_done()` | Mark items resolved |
| `cluster` | `cmd_cluster_dispatch()` | Create, merge, annotate clusters |
| `triage` | `cmd_plan_triage()` | 4-stage LLM-driven triage workflow |

**Auto-reconciliation:**
- `WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID` auto-resolved when `plan` command runs (self-healing)

---

### 5. **Git Integration** (`desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py`)

**Limited Git Usage:**
```python
def _warn_uncommitted_changes() -> None:
    try:
        r = subprocess.run(
            ["git", "status", "--porcelain"], 
            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5
        )
        if r.stdout.strip():
            # Warn user to commit before fix
            print("⚠ You have uncommitted changes. Consider running:")
            print("  git add -A && git commit -m 'pre-fix checkpoint' && git push")
    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
        return
```

**Scope:**
- Only runs `git status --porcelain` to detect uncommitted changes
- **No git add/commit** — just warnings
- **No git pull/push** — workflow is "work locally, commit manually"
- **No branch management**
- Fails gracefully if git unavailable (timeout, OSError)

---

### 6. **Work Queue Architecture** (`desloppify/engine/_work_queue/`, `desloppify/engine/work_queue.py`)

**Queue Building:**
```python
build_work_queue(state, options=QueueBuildOptions) → WorkQueueResult
```

**QueueBuildOptions:**
- `count` — limit results
- `status` — filter (open, fixed, deferred, all)
- `scope` — pattern matching
- `include_subjective` — include dimension reviews
- `subjective_threshold` — score cutoff for subjective items
- `chronic` — filter by persistence
- `explain` — add explain text to items
- `plan` — apply living plan ordering
- `include_skipped` — show skipped items
- `cluster` — drill into specific cluster
- `collapse_clusters` — collapse auto-clusters to meta-items

**Ranking:**
- `item_sort_key()` determines sort order
- `enrich_with_impact()` adds dimension impact context
- `group_queue_items()` groups by "item" type

**Synthetic Items:**
- Triage stages: `triage::observe`, `triage::reflect`, `triage::organize`, `triage::commit`
- Workflow: `workflow::create-plan`
- Subjective dimensions: `subjective::{slugified_name}`

**Plan Integration:**
- `apply_plan_order()` reorders items per living plan
- `collapse_clusters()` hides manual clusters (unless focused)
- **New IDs tracked:** set of findings added since last triage

---

### 7. **Plan Persistence & Reconciliation** (`desloppify/engine/_plan/`)

**Plan Schema** (`.desloppify/plan.json`):
```python
{
  "version": 7,
  "created": "2026-03-03T...",
  "updated": "2026-03-03T...",
  "queue_order": [],          # prioritized finding IDs
  "skipped": {},              # {fid: {kind, reason, note, attestation, ...}}
  "active_cluster": None,     # focused cluster name
  "overrides": {},            # {fid: {description, note, cluster, ...}}
  "clusters": {},             # {name: {finding_ids, description, action, ...}}
  "superseded": {},           # {fid: {original_id, status, candidates, ...}}
  "promoted_ids": [],         # user-moved items
  "plan_start_scores": {},    # frozen scores at plan creation
  "execution_log": [],        # timestamped action log
  "epic_triage_meta": {}      # triage engine state
}
```

**Reconciliation After Scan** (`reconcile.py`):
- Called post-scan (best-effort, non-blocking)
- **Supersedes findings** that no longer exist or are no longer open
- **Prunes old superseded entries** (90-day TTL)
- **Resurfaces stale temporary skips**
- **Cleans epic clusters** of dead findings
- **Logs all changes** to execution_log

**Reconciliation After Review Import** (`sync_plan_after_review_import()`):
- Appends new review finding IDs to queue_order
- Injects triage stages if review findings changed
- Returns `ReviewImportSyncResult` (new_ids, added_to_queue, triage_injected)

**Stale Dimension Sync** (`stale_dimensions.py`):
- **Sync unscored dimensions** — prepend placeholder dimensions to front (onboarding priority)
- **Sync stale dimensions** — append stale reviews to back when objective backlog is zero
- **Sync triage needed** — injects 4 triage stages when review findings change
- **Sync create-plan needed** — injects workflow item when reviews complete + objective work exists
- Uses **finding snapshot hash** (SHA256 of open review IDs) to detect changes

---

### 8. **State Management & Persistence**

**State File Location:**
- `.desloppify/state-{language}.json` (language-specific)

**Key State Attributes:**
- `findings` — {id: {detector, status, file, summary, detail, ...}}
- `dimension_scores` — {dimension: {score, detectors: {...}}}
- `subjective_assessments` — {dimension_key: {placeholder, score, ...}}
- `scan_count` — incremental counter
- `scan_path` — last scanned directory
- `review_cache` — holistic review metadata
- `assessment_import_audit` — review import history

**Atomic Persistence:**
- Uses same `safe_write_text()` pattern as config
- Backups created before write
- Validation on load; falls back to backup if corrupted

---

### 9. **Key Patterns & Behaviors**

**Execution Log:**
- All plan mutations logged to `execution_log` with timestamps, actor ("user"/"system"/"agent"), and details
- Capped at `execution_log_max_entries` (default 10,000)
- Used for audit trail + decision justification

**Synthetic IDs:**
- Non-finding IDs used for workflow coordination:
  - `triage::*` — 4 stages
  - `workflow::*` — system workflows
  - `subjective::*` — dimension reviews
- Excluded from reconciliation (manager by `stale_dimensions.py`)

**Finding Status Lifecycle:**
- `open` → `fixed` (auto-resolved) / `suppressed` / `deferred`
- Skip kinds: temporary, permanent (wontfix), false_positive, triaged_out
- Temporary skips can resurface after N scans

**Zone Classification:**
- Path-based (production, test, config, generated, script, vendor)
- Can be overridden in config `zone_overrides`

---

### 10. **Configuration Validation & Migration**

**Type Coercion:**
- Integers: "never" → 0
- Booleans: "true"/"1"/"yes" → true; "false"/"0"/"no" → false
- Badge path: must be a filename, not a directory

**Schema Versioning:**
- Plan v7 (latest)
- Auto-migrations: deferred→skipped, epics→clusters, synthesis→triage

---

### Summary of User Workflows

**Primary Loop:**
1. `desloppify scan` → updates `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` + `.desloppify/config.json`
2. `desloppify plan` → generates prioritized queue + applies overrides
3. `desloppify next` → shows top 1 item with fix guidance
4. User fixes + runs resolve command
5. `desloppify scan` again (reconciles plan, detects new findings)

**Plan Manipulation:**
- `desloppify plan move <id> <position>` — reorder
- `desloppify plan describe <pattern> <text>` — add context
- `desloppify plan cluster create <name>` — group related findings
- `desloppify plan triage` — LLM-driven clustering of review findings

**No Interactive Prompts:**
- All input via CLI arguments
- Triage workflow is form-driven (stages with gates for text enrichment)
- Text enrichment collected via stdin redirection or file args

---

**Absolute Paths for Key Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/config.py` — config API
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/file_paths.py` — path utilities
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/persistence.py` — plan load/save
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile.py` — post-scan sync
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` — queue building
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py` — git check
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` — entry point

> DEVELOPER

You are designing a new "commit integration" feature for the desloppify CLI tool. Based on the codebase exploration below, design a detailed implementation plan.

## Current State
- Python CLI tool using argparse (no click/typer)
- No interactive prompts currently — all input via CLI args
- Plan data model: `PlanModel` TypedDict in `engine/_plan/schema.py` with fields: version, queue_order, skipped, clusters, overrides, execution_log, etc.
- Config: `.desloppify/config.json` managed by `core/config.py` with typed settings
- Resolution flow: `resolve` command → state update → plan purge + execution_log entry → render score delta + "run next" hint
- Execution log entries have: timestamp, action, finding_ids, cluster_name, actor, note, detail
- Atomic persistence (temp + os.replace + .bak) for all JSON files
- Only existing git usage: `git status --porcelain` in fix/apply_flow.py for a warning
- Clusters group findings; active_cluster focuses the queue
- `purge_ids()` removes resolved findings from queue/clusters

## Requirements
1. **Post-resolution commit prompt**: When user resolves task(s) via `resolve`, show commit guidance
2. **Commit tracking**: Record which findings were committed, to which git SHA, on which branch
3. **Bundling logic**: Show uncommitted previous resolved tasks and upcoming next tasks, help user decide commit grouping
4. **Branch config**: Store default branch name in config, allow overriding
5. **Commit rationale**: Encourage including resolution notes/attestation in commit messages
6. **Goal**: Keep users thinking about committing throughout the process

## Design Constraints
- The tool doesn't currently do interactive stdin prompts. Consider whether to add them or keep the CLI-arg pattern.
- Must work when git is not available (graceful degradation)
- Must not break existing workflows
- Must store commit metadata durably in plan data (not just execution_log)
- Should feel natural in the scan → plan → next → resolve → next loop

## Things to Design

### A. Data Model Changes
Where to store commit metadata? Options:
1. New top-level field on PlanModel (e.g., `commit_log`)
2. On execution_log entry detail dict
3. New field per-finding in state
Think about: what needs to be queryable? We need "which findings are uncommitted?" and "what commit did finding X go to?"

### B. Config Changes  
What settings to add to config.json? Think about:
- Default branch name
- Whether commit prompts are enabled/disabled
- Commit message template

### C. CLI Interface
How does the user interact? Think about:
- Post-resolve output: what exactly is shown?
- New `commit` subcommand? Or integrated into resolve?
- How to bundle: `resolve --commit` flag? Or separate `desloppify commit` command?
- How to specify "commit this with the next task too" (deferring commit)

### D. Git Operations
What git operations does the tool perform?
- Just suggestions? Or actual `git add/commit`?
- Branch creation/switching?
- How to handle uncommitted changes detection?

### E. Render Changes
What changes to the resolve/next output to encourage committing?

### F. The "bundle" concept
How do we handle:
- "Commit just this task"
- "Bundle with previously-resolved-but-uncommitted tasks"  
- "Wait and bundle with the next task"
The user needs to see what's uncommitted and what's coming up.

Please provide a concrete, detailed implementation plan with specific file paths, data structures, and function signatures. Consider trade-offs and recommend the best approach.

```
    99→def empty_plan() -> PlanModel:
   100→    """Return a new empty plan payload."""
   101→    now = utc_now()
   102→    return {
   103→        "version": PLAN_VERSION,
   104→        "created": now,
   105→        "updated": now,
   106→        "queue_order": [],
   107→        "deferred": [],
   108→        "skipped": {},
   109→        "active_cluster": None,
   110→        "overrides": {},
   111→        "clusters": {},
   112→        "superseded": {},
   113→        "promoted_ids": [],
   114→        "plan_start_scores": {},
   115→        "execution_log": [],
   116→        "epic_triage_meta": {},
   117→    }
   118→
   119→
   120→def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   121→    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.
   122→
   123→    Handles migration from v1 (deferred list) to v2 (skipped dict),
   124→    v3 (top-level epics) to v4 (epics unified into clusters),
   125→    v5 (gates) to v6 (unified queue),
   126→    and v6 (synthesis naming) to v7 (triage naming).
   127→    """
   128→    defaults = empty_plan()
   129→    for key, value in defaults.items():
   130→        plan.setdefault(key, value)
   131→
   132→    _ensure_container_types(plan)
   133→    _migrate_deferred_to_skipped(plan)
   134→    _migrate_epics_to_clusters(plan)
   135→    _normalize_cluster_defaults(plan)
   136→    _migrate_v5_to_v6(plan)
   137→    _migrate_synthesis_to_triage(plan)
   138→
   139→
   140→def triage_clusters(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Cluster]:
   141→    """Return clusters whose name starts with ``EPIC_PREFIX``."""
   142→    return {
   143→        name: cluster
   144→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   145→        if name.startswith(EPIC_PREFIX)
   146→    }
   147→
   148→
   149→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   150→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   151→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   152→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   153→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   154→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   155→
   156→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   157→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   158→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   159→    if overlap:
   160→        raise ValueError(
   161→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   162→        )
   163→
   164→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   165→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   166→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   167→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   168→            raise ValueError(
   169→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   170→            )
   171→
   172→
   173→__all__ = [
   174→    "EPIC_PREFIX",
```

```
   100→def default_config() -> dict[str, Any]:
   101→    """Return a config dict with all keys set to their defaults."""
   102→    return {k: copy.deepcopy(v.default) for k, v in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items()}
   103→
   104→
   105→def _coerce_target_strict_score(value: object) -> tuple[int, bool]:
   106→    """Coerce target strict score and report whether it is in range."""
   107→    try:
   108→        parsed = int(value)
   109→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   110→        return MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE, False
   111→    valid = MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE <= parsed <= MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
   112→    return parsed, valid
   113→
   114→
   115→def load_config(path: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
   116→    """Load config from disk, auto-migrating from state files if needed.
   117→
   118→    Fills missing keys with defaults. If no config.json exists, attempts
   119→    migration from state-*.json files.
   120→    """
   121→    p = path or _default_config_file()
   122→    if p.exists():
   123→        try:
   124→            config = json.loads(p.read_text())
   125→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
   126→            config = {}
   127→    else:
   128→        # First run — try migrating from state files
   129→        config = _migrate_from_state_files(p)
   130→
   131→    changed = False
   132→
   133→    # Fill missing keys with defaults
   134→    for key, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items():
   135→        if key not in config:
   136→            config[key] = copy.deepcopy(schema.default)
   137→            changed = True
   138→        elif key == "badge_path":
   139→            try:
   140→                normalized = _validate_badge_path(str(config[key]))
   141→                if normalized != config[key]:
   142→                    config[key] = normalized
   143→                    changed = True
   144→            except ValueError:
   145→                config[key] = copy.deepcopy(schema.default)
   146→                changed = True
   147→
   148→    if changed and p.exists():
   149→        try:
   150→            save_config(config, p)
   151→        except OSError as exc:
   152→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"persist migrated config to {p}", exc)
   153→
   154→    return config
   155→
   156→
   157→def save_config(config: dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
   158→    """Save config to disk atomically."""
   159→    p = path or _default_config_file()
   160→    safe_write_text(p, json.dumps(config, indent=2) + "\n")
   161→
   162→
   163→def add_ignore_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
   164→    """Append a pattern to the ignore list (deduplicates)."""
   165→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   166→    if pattern not in ignores:
   167→        ignores.append(pattern)
   168→
   169→
   170→def add_exclude_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
   171→    """Append a pattern to the exclude list (deduplicates)."""
   172→    excludes = config.setdefault("exclude", [])
   173→    if pattern not in excludes:
   174→        excludes.append(pattern)
   175→
   176→
   177→def set_ignore_metadata(config: dict, pattern: str, *, note: str, added_at: str) -> None:
   178→    """Record note + timestamp for an ignore pattern."""
   179→    meta = config.setdefault("ignore_metadata", {})
   180→    if not isinstance(meta, dict):
   181→        meta = {}
   182→        config["ignore_metadata"] = meta
   183→    meta[pattern] = {"note": note, "added_at": added_at}
   184→
   185→
   186→def _validate_badge_path(raw: str) -> str:
   187→    """Require badge_path to point to a filename (root or nested path)."""
   188→    value = raw.strip()
   189→    path = Path(value)
   190→    if (
   191→        not value
   192→        or value.endswith(("/", "\\"))
   193→        or path.name in {"", ".", ".."}
   194→    ):
   195→        raise ValueError(
   196→            "Expected file path for badge_path "
   197→            f"(example: scorecard.png or assets/scorecard.png), got: {raw}"
   198→        )
   199→    return value
   200→
   201→
   202→def _set_int_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   203→    if raw.lower() == "never":
   204→        config[key] = 0
   205→    else:
   206→        config[key] = int(raw)
   207→    if key != "target_strict_score":
   208→        return
   209→    target_strict_score, target_valid = _coerce_target_strict_score(config[key])
   210→    if not target_valid:
   211→        raise ValueError(
   212→            f"Expected integer {MIN_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE}-{MAX_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE} "
   213→            f"for {key}, got: {raw}"
   214→        )
   215→    config[key] = target_strict_score
   216→
   217→
   218→def _set_bool_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   219→    normalized = raw.lower()
   220→    if normalized in ("true", "1", "yes"):
   221→        config[key] = True
   222→        return
   223→    if normalized in ("false", "0", "no"):
   224→        config[key] = False
   225→        return
   226→    raise ValueError(f"Expected true/false for {key}, got: {raw}")
   227→
   228→
   229→def _set_str_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   230→    if key == "badge_path":
   231→        config[key] = _validate_badge_path(raw)
   232→        return
   233→    config[key] = raw
   234→
   235→
   236→def _set_list_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   237→    config.setdefault(key, [])
   238→    if raw not in config[key]:
   239→        config[key].append(raw)
   240→
   241→
   242→def set_config_value(config: dict, key: str, raw: str) -> None:
   243→    """Parse and set a config value from a raw string.
   244→
   245→    Handles special cases:
   246→    - "never" → 0 for age keys
   247→    - "true"/"false" for bools
   248→    """
   249→    if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   250→        raise KeyError(f"Unknown config key: {key}")
   251→
   252→    schema = CONFIG_SCHEMA[key]
   253→
   254→    if schema.type is int:
   255→        _set_int_config_value(config, key, raw)
   256→        return
   257→    if schema.type is bool:
   258→        _set_bool_config_value(config, key, raw)
   259→        return
   260→    if schema.type is str:
   261→        _set_str_config_value(config, key, raw)
   262→        return
   263→    if schema.type is list:
   264→        _set_list_config_value(config, key, raw)
   265→        return
   266→    if schema.type is dict:
   267→        raise ValueError(f"Cannot set dict key '{key}' via CLI — use subcommands")
   268→    config[key] = raw
   269→
   270→
   271→def unset_config_value(config: dict, key: str) -> None:
   272→    """Reset a config key to its default value."""
   273→    if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   274→        raise KeyError(f"Unknown config key: {key}")
   275→    config[key] = copy.deepcopy(CONFIG_SCHEMA[key].default)
   276→
   277→
   278→def config_for_query(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   279→    """Return a sanitized config dict suitable for query.json."""
   280→    return {k: config.get(k, schema.default) for k, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items()}
   281→
   282→
   283→def _merge_config_value(config: dict, key: str, value: object) -> None:
   284→    """Merge a config value into the target dict."""
   285→    if key not in config:
   286→        config[key] = copy.deepcopy(value)
   287→        return
   288→    if isinstance(value, list) and isinstance(config[key], list):
   289→        for item in value:
   290→            if item not in config[key]:
   291→                config[key].append(item)
   292→        return
   293→    if isinstance(value, dict) and isinstance(config[key], dict):
   294→        for dk, dv in value.items():
   295→            if dk not in config[key]:
   296→                config[key][dk] = copy.deepcopy(dv)
   297→        return
   298→
   299→
   300→def _load_state_file_payload(path: Path) -> dict | None:
   301→    try:
   302→        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
   303→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   304→        logger.debug("Skipping unreadable state file %s: %s", path, exc)
   305→        return None
   306→    if isinstance(payload, dict):
   307→        return payload
   308→    return None
   309→
   310→
   311→def _merge_legacy_state_config(config: dict, old_config: dict) -> None:
   312→    for key, value in old_config.items():
   313→        if key not in CONFIG_SCHEMA:
   314→            continue
   315→        _merge_config_value(config, key, value)
   316→
   317→
   318→def _strip_config_from_state_file(path: Path, state_data: dict) -> None:
   319→    if "config" not in state_data:
   320→        return
   321→    del state_data["config"]
   322→    try:
   323→        safe_write_text(path, json.dumps(state_data, indent=2) + "\n")
   324→    except OSError as exc:
   325→        log_best_effort_failure(
   326→            logger,
   327→            f"rewrite state file {path} after config migration",
   328→            exc,
   329→        )
   330→
   331→
   332→def _migrate_single_state_file(config: dict, path: Path) -> bool:
   333→    state_data = _load_state_file_payload(path)
   334→    if state_data is None:
   335→        return False
   336→    old_config = state_data.get("config")
   337→    if not isinstance(old_config, dict) or not old_config:
   338→        return False
   339→
   340→    _merge_legacy_state_config(config, old_config)
   341→    _strip_config_from_state_file(path, state_data)
   342→    return True
   343→
   344→
   345→def _migrate_from_state_files(config_path: Path) -> dict:
   346→    """Migrate config keys from state-*.json files into config.json.
   347→
   348→    Reads state["config"] from all state files, merges them (union for
   349→    lists, merge for dicts), writes config.json, and strips "config" from
   350→    the state files.
   351→    """
   352→    config: dict = {}
   353→    state_dir = config_path.parent
   354→    if not state_dir.exists():
   355→        return config
   356→
   357→    state_files = list(state_dir.glob("state-*.json")) + list(
   358→        state_dir.glob("state.json")
   359→    )
   360→    migrated_any = False
   361→    for sf in state_files:
   362→        migrated_any = _migrate_single_state_file(config, sf) or migrated_any
   363→
   364→    if migrated_any and config:
   365→        try:
   366→            save_config(config, config_path)
   367→        except OSError as exc:
   368→            log_best_effort_failure(
   369→                logger, f"save migrated config to {config_path}", exc
   370→            )
   371→
   372→    return config
   373→
```

```
1→"""Resolve command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import show_score_with_plan_context
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.guardrails import require_triage_current_or_exit
    13→from desloppify.core import config as config_mod  # noqa: F401 (compat export)
    14→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    15→from desloppify.engine.plan import append_log_entry, has_living_plan, load_plan, purge_ids, save_plan
    16→from desloppify.intelligence import narrative as narrative_mod
    17→from desloppify.state import coerce_assessment_score
    18→
    19→from .apply import _resolve_all_patterns, _write_resolve_query_entry
    20→from .ignore_cmd import cmd_ignore_pattern
    21→from .persist import _save_state_or_exit
    22→from .queue_guard import _check_queue_order_guard
    23→from .render import (
    24→    _print_next_command,
    25→    _print_resolve_summary,
    26→    _print_subjective_reset_hint,
    27→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning,
    28→)
    29→from .selection import (
    30→    ResolveQueryContext,
    31→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation,
    32→    _previous_score_snapshot,
    33→    _validate_resolve_inputs,
    34→    show_note_length_requirement,
    35→    validate_note_length,
    36→)
    37→
    38→
    39→def cmd_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    40→    """Resolve finding(s) matching one or more patterns."""
    41→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    42→    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)
    43→
    44→    if args.status == "fixed":
    45→        note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    46→        if not validate_note_length(note):
    47→            show_note_length_requirement(note)
    48→            return
    49→
    50→    state_file = state_path(args)
    51→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    52→
    53→    if _check_queue_order_guard(state, args.patterns, args.status):
    54→        return
    55→
    56→    if args.status == "fixed":
    57→        require_triage_current_or_exit(
    58→            state=state,
    59→            bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
    60→            attest=getattr(args, "attest", "") or "",
    61→        )
    62→
    63→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
    64→        state,
    65→        args,
    66→        attestation=attestation,
    67→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,
    68→    )
    69→    prev = _previous_score_snapshot(state)
    70→    prev_subjective_scores = {
    71→        str(dim): (coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0)
    72→        for dim, payload in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}).items()
    73→        if isinstance(dim, str)
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    all_resolved = _resolve_all_patterns(state, args, attestation=attestation)
    77→    if not all_resolved:
    78→        status_label = "resolved" if args.status == "open" else "open"
    79→        print(colorize(f"No {status_label} findings matching: {' '.join(args.patterns)}", "yellow"))
    80→        return
    81→
    82→    _save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
    83→
    84→    try:
    85→        if has_living_plan():
    86→            plan = load_plan()
    87→            purged = purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)
    88→            append_log_entry(
    89→                plan,
    90→                "resolve",
    91→                finding_ids=all_resolved,
    92→                actor="user",
    93→                note=getattr(args, "note", None),
    94→                detail={"status": args.status, "attestation": attestation},
    95→            )
    96→            if purged:
    97→                save_plan(plan)
    98→                print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: {purged} item(s) removed from queue.", "dim"))
    99→            else:
   100→                save_plan(plan)
   101→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   102→        print(colorize("  Warning: could not update living plan.", "yellow"), file=sys.stderr)
   103→
   104→    _print_resolve_summary(status=args.status, all_resolved=all_resolved)
   105→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning(
   106→        state,
   107→        status=args.status,
   108→        resolved_count=len(all_resolved),
   109→    )
   110→    show_score_with_plan_context(state, prev)
   111→    _print_subjective_reset_hint(
   112→        args=args,
   113→        state=state,
   114→        all_resolved=all_resolved,
   115→        prev_subjective_scores=prev_subjective_scores,
   116→    )
   117→
   118→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   119→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   120→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
   121→        state,
   122→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="resolve"),
   123→    )
   124→    if narrative.get("milestone"):
   125→        print(colorize(f"  → {narrative['milestone']}", "green"))
   126→
   127→    next_command = _print_next_command(state)
   128→    _write_resolve_query_entry(
   129→        ResolveQueryContext(
   130→            patterns=args.patterns,
   131→            status=args.status,
   132→            resolved=all_resolved,
   133→            next_command=next_command,
   134→            prev_overall=prev.overall,
   135→            prev_objective=prev.objective,
   136→            prev_strict=prev.strict,
   137→            prev_verified=prev.verified,
   138→            attestation=attestation,
   139→            narrative=narrative,
   140→            state=state,
   141→        )
   142→    )
   143→
   144→
   145→__all__ = ["_check_queue_order_guard", "cmd_ignore_pattern", "cmd_resolve"]
   146→
```
